=== PROMPT VARIANT ===
r1

=== PROMPT ===
You will be given a competitive programming problem.
There is an integer sequence x = (x_1, \ldots, x_N), which is initialized with x_1 = \cdots = x_N = 0.
You will perform M operations on this integer sequence. In the i-th operation, you are given an integer pair (L_i, R_i) such that 1 \leq L_i \leq R_i \leq N, and you must perform exactly one of the following three operations:

- Operation 0: Do nothing. This operation incurs a cost of 0.
- Operation 1: For each integer j with 1 \leq j \leq N, if L_i \leq j \leq R_i holds, set x_j = 1. This operation incurs a cost of 1.
- Operation 2: For each integer j with 1 \leq j \leq N, if L_i \leq j \leq R_i does not hold, set x_j = 1. This operation incurs a cost of 1.

Your goal is to make x_1 = \cdots = x_N = 1 hold at the end. Determine whether this goal can be achieved. If it can be achieved, present one way to achieve it where the total cost of the operations is minimized.

Input

The input is given from Standard Input in the following format:
N M
L_1 R_1
\vdots
L_M R_M

Output

If the goal is not achievable, print -1.
If the goal is achievable, print one way to achieve it where the total cost of the operations is minimized, in the following format, where K is the minimum total cost of the operations, and \mathrm{op}_i is the type of operation (0, 1, or 2) chosen for the i-th operation.
K
\mathrm{op}_1 \cdots \mathrm{op}_M

If there are multiple ways that minimize the total cost, printing any one of them is accepted.

Constraints


- 1 \leq N \leq 1000000
- 1 \leq M \leq 200000
- 1 \leq L_i \leq R_i \leq N
- All input values are integers.

Sample Input 1

5 4
2 4
3 5
1 4
2 5

Sample Output 1

2
2 0 1 0

In the sample output, x changes as follows:

- Initially, x = (0,0,0,0,0).
- In the 1st operation, Operation 2 is performed. x_1 and x_5 become 1, so x = (1,0,0,0,1).
- In the 2nd operation, Operation 0 is performed. x remains (1,0,0,0,1).
- In the 3rd operation, Operation 1 is performed. x_1, x_2, x_3, x_4 become 1, so x = (1,1,1,1,1).
- In the 4th operation, Operation 0 is performed. x remains (1,1,1,1,1).

Sample Input 2

5 4
1 3
1 5
2 4
3 5

Sample Output 2

1
0 1 0 0

Sample Input 3

5 2
1 3
2 5

Sample Output 3

2
1 1

Sample Input 4

5 2
1 3
2 4

Sample Output 4

-1

Reason in a commit-implement-recheck way.

A productive rhythm for this task usually looks like this:
- Isolate the decisive constraints, the target complexity, and at most one or two approaches that are still truly plausible.
- If two candidates remain, compare them only on the decisive constraint and commit quickly to the stronger one instead of keeping a wide search open.
- Before coding, keep only a tiny implementation ledger for the chosen plan: main invariant, key state or transition, and the one or two edge cases most likely to fail.
- Move into code as soon as that ledger is clear. After coding starts, keep reasoning attached to the current draft rather than reopening the high-level search.
- After the first complete draft, do one short implementation-check-adjust-check loop on the same draft: test the key edge case, patch locally if needed, and recheck.
- Reopen the main design only if a concrete counterexample breaks the core invariant. Otherwise prefer the local patch and keep closing.
- Once the current draft, invariant, and short recheck still agree, finalize the solution.

Try to avoid patterns like these:
- spending too long comparing nearby plans before committing to one workable approach;
- returning from code to broad algorithm discussion without a concrete contradiction;
- turning one local doubt into a full restart when the current draft only needs a small patch.

Output only the final Python code enclosed in a code block.

Let's understand coverage by selected operations. Let S be set of selected indices, and for each i in S choose type t_i. For a position p, define:

Let's define for selected S, if we choose a subset T ⊆ S to be type1, rest type2. Then p uncovered iff all type1 ops in S have p∉I_i AND all type2 ops in S have p∈I_i. That is:

So p is uncovered exactly if T ⊆ O_p (ops whose interval does NOT contain p) and S\T ⊆ C_p (ops whose interval contains p), where O_p = {i: p∉I_i}, C_p = {i: p∈I_i}. Thus T must be subset of O_p and S\T subset of C_p, equivalently T contains no ops containing p and S\T contains no ops not containing p. Since S = T ∪ (S\T), this means:
- All ops in T (type1) must not contain p.
- All ops in S\T (type2) must contain p.
So p is uncovered iff T ⊆ O_p and S\T ⊆ C_p.

Equivalently, for p to be covered, either some type1 op contains p or some type2 op does not contain p.

We have M intervals. Need choose binary labels for subset. Equivalent to choose S1 (type1) and S2 (type2) disjoint. Let J = ∩_{i∈S2} I_i (with J=[1,N] if S2 empty). Need J ⊆ U = ∪_{i∈S1} I_i. Cost |S1|+|S2|.

Since S1 and S2 disjoint, but if an operation is unused, can be in neither. We can choose S2 to define J; then S1 must cover J using intervals not in S2. However, if an interval is in S2, it cannot help cover J, but its interval contains J (by definition intersection) actually every S2 interval contains J. Maybe we can think of selecting a "cut" or interval J to be covered by type1, while all type2 intervals contain J. For a chosen J=[A,B] (possibly empty), S2 can be any subset of operations whose intervals contain J? Wait intersection of S2 equals J exactly? If S2 intervals all contain J, their intersection is some J' ⊇ J (maybe larger). If J' larger, type2 leaves larger hole; type1 must cover J'. But type1 covering J' implies covering J. So for feasibility with cost, we can choose S2 and let J' be actual intersection. We don't need predefine J. But we can view: choose S2, let J = intersection. Then S2 intervals all contain J. Conversely, if we choose an interval J and S2 subset of ops containing J, intersection may be larger than J; type1 must cover intersection. To minimize, maybe choose S2 with small intersection.

Also S1 intervals must cover J. Since S1 ops are not in S2. But if an op contains J and is in S2, not available. If an op contains J and unused, can be used as type1.

Actually op2 [1,N] covers nothing, not useful. J empty means max L > min R. For intervals, this means there exist two selected intervals with L_a > R_b? More generally max L > min R. Pseudo:

import sys
def main():
    input = sys.stdin.readline
    N,M = map(int,input().split())
    L=[0]*M; R=[0]*M
    has_full = -1
    maxL = -1; minR = N+1
    maxL_idx = -1; minR_idx = -1
    hasL1 = -1; hasRN = -1
    for i in range(M):
        l,r = map(int,input().split())
        L[i]=l; R[i]=r
        if l==1 and r==N:
            has_full = i
        if l==1:
            hasL1 = i if hasL1==-1 else hasL1
        if r==N:
            hasRN = i if hasRN==-1 else hasRN
        if l > maxL:
            maxL = l; maxL_idx = i
        if r < minR:
            minR = r; minR_idx = i

    if has_full != -1:
        ops=[0]*M; ops[has_full]=1
        print(1); print(' '.join(map(str,ops))); return

    if maxL > minR:
        ops=[0]*M
        ops[maxL_idx]=2
        ops[minR_idx]=2
        print(2); print(' '.join(map(str,ops))); return

    # common intersection
    # cost2 check
    if hasL1 != -1 and hasRN != -1:
        ops=[0]*M
        ops[hasL1]=1
        ops[hasRN]=1
        print(2); print(' '.join(map(str,ops))); return

    # containment check
    # Need find if exists i,j distinct with I_i subset I_j.
    # If yes, output cost2 with contained type2, containing type1.
    # Since no full, no L1/RN? maybe hasL1 or hasRN individually.
    order = sorted(range(M), key=lambda i: (L[i], -R[i]))
    maxR = -1
    maxR_idx = -1
    contained_idx = -1
    containing_idx = -1
    for idx in order:
        if maxR >= R[idx]:
            contained_idx = idx
            containing_idx = maxR_idx
            break
        if R[idx] > maxR:
            maxR = R[idx]
            maxR_idx = idx
    if contained_idx != -1:
        ops=[0]*M
        ops[contained_idx]=2
        ops[containing_idx]=1
        print(2); print(' '.join(map(str,ops))); return

    if M >= 3:
        # output 3
        ops=[0]*M
        ops[maxL_idx]=2
        ops[minR_idx]=2
        # choose r not maxL_idx and not minR_idx
        # Since M>=3 and maxL_idx != minR_idx? Need ensure.
        # If maxL_idx == minR_idx, then containment would have been found? But to be safe:
        if maxL_idx == minR_idx:
            # This case should not happen if containment false, but handle:
            # choose any r != maxL_idx; cost2? Actually if maxL_idx==minR_idx, I_p=[a,b], any other contains it, so containment true. So not here.
            pass
        for i in range(M):
            if i != maxL_idx and i != minR_idx:
                ops[i]=1
                break
        print(3); print(' '.join(map(str,ops))); return
    else:
        print(-1); return

- 2,2: coverage = I^c ∪ J^c = (I∩J)^c. Need I∩J=∅, impossible because all intervals intersect.
